AUDIT — Last audited: 30 October 2026

Scope. This is an independent audit of the North Dakota Cannabis & Hemp Research — Complete (Parts 0–12) file dated 30 October 2026. The auditor did not write the original. Every factual claim was checked section by section against primary sources only: the North Dakota Century Code (including Chapters 19-03.1 and 19-24.1) and bill pages on ndlegis.gov, the North Dakota Administrative Code, the HHS Division of Medical Marijuana (including its reports), the Department of Agriculture, the Office of State Tax Commissioner, the Attorney General's official opinions, the Secretary of State for ballot measures and certified results, official tribal government sources and codes, and official county, city and township sites and codes. News, law-firm blogs, trade press and AI summaries were never used to confirm a figure; where only a secondary source exists the verdict is Unverified.

Federal baseline (as given, not re-audited). Split scheduling under a final rule at 91 FR 22714 (effective 28 April 2026): FDA-approved products and state-licensed MEDICAL marijuana are Schedule III; all other marijuana, including all adult-use, remains Schedule I; the broader 2024 proposal is NOT final. Treasury has said rescheduling generally removes 280E as a bar for businesses no longer dealing in Schedule I/II substances (guidance forthcoming); adult-use remains fully subject to 280E. Hemp: PL 119-37 §781 takes legal effect 12 November 2026, but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026 to only the provisions excluding cannabinoids that are not naturally produced or are synthesized; the full total-THC definition and 0.4 mg per container cap apply after 11 December 2026. USDA has delayed enforcement of the DEA-registered-laboratory hemp testing requirement until 31 December 2026. The federal medical-marijuana appropriations rider is in force through 11 December 2026. No federal cannabis banking law has been enacted. The report states this baseline correctly and applies Schedule III / 280E relief to North Dakota's state-licensed medical licensees only; any non-medical possession is treated as Schedule I. North Dakota's own schedule position under NDCC §19-03.1-02(4) and tribal “state-licensed” status both remain Verify — see rows below.

Verdict totals (sum exactly to the rows audited). Total rows: 96 — Confirmed: 40; Corrected: 28; Removed: 4; Unverified: 24.

How the rewrite was built. Every section and all detail from the original are kept. Only what the audit changed was changed, in place, everywhere it appears. No outdated value is left standing in the main text, and no “earlier research said X” note is left in the main text — the audit table below is the record of what changed and why. Verify / Unavailable flags remain only on items still unresolved after the audit. The 3 November 2026 election had not occurred on 30 October 2026, so no 2026 result is reported. Place-name discipline is kept throughout: Fargo, ND is not Moorhead, MN; Grand Forks, ND is not East Grand Forks, MN; Williston, Dickinson, Jamestown, Grafton, Rugby and Devils Lake in ND are not their same-name counterparts elsewhere; every township is identified with its county. No personal phone number or email for an individual licence holder is reproduced; agency public contacts only.

Headline resolutions (read before using the rewrite). (1) Plant surcharge: $5,000 payable per 500 plants over 1,000, statutory ceiling $7,500 (SB 2201, 2023) — the Run 2 $10,000 adoption is reversed. (2) Certification fees are date-stamped: through 31 Oct 2026 $75,000/$60,000/$40,000/$20,000; from 1 Nov 2026 $110,000/$90,000/$70,000/$50,000. (3) Patient figures are CARDS, not people: 10,581 cards at 30 June 2024 (9,934 + 164 + 483); 10,982 cards issued on the HHS hub counter at 8 Oct 2026. (4) Ballot percentages for 2018/2022/2024 are reported figures whose SOS canvass tables remain Verify. (5) First Light (Browns Valley, MN) is not a North Dakota dispensary and not ND state-licensed. (6) Hemp: no purchase age in ND law, no lawful THC-beverage category, no ND adoption of the federal 0.4 mg cap, and no current NDDA licence-count total from a public source.

Nonresident section is §19-24.1-03.3, not §19-24.1-03.1 (which is Veterans). §19-24.1-03.3: same-state home card + department-approved ID in lieu of certification; department issues within 30 calendar days of approval; card valid 60 days. HHS instructions (Mar 2026) add: 19+, 49 states + DC + territories, $40 online only, 2–4 weeks, home ID + card must remain valid for the full 60 days. This is NOT counter-recognition of a home card alone, and does NOT authorise bringing out-of-state product into ND.

Use $5,000 payable, $7,500 ceiling. Statute and rule do not conflict once cap vs. amount-collected is distinguished. The 1 Nov 2026 step-up did not change the plant fee.

These are different measures at different dates, not a conflict. Part 7 §7.4Hemp licence types ONLY grower + processor; grower $100/$200/$300/$350 by lots, processor $200; statutory cap $350; background-check fee $40.00Confirmedhttps://www.ndda.nd.gov/divisions/plant-industries/hempConfirmed. NDCC §4.1-18.1-02: any person desiring to grow or process hemp applies to the Agriculture Commissioner. NDDA lists only Hemp License Application (SFN 58476) and Hemp Processor License Application (SFN 61613); no handler/transporter/distributor/retailer/finished-product licence appears. A lot is a contiguous area with the same variety/strain. NDCC §4.1-18.1-03 caps the licence fee at $350. Background check $40.00 (NDDA page, SFN 60688 + 2 fingerprint sets); $41.25 is secondary only — see Removed row.
Part 7 §7.4Hemp testing fee $125 — per lot / per sample / per inspectionUnverifiedhttps://www.legis.nd.gov/cencode/t04-1c18-1.pdfNDCC §4.1-18.1-04.2(1) authorises up to $125 to inspect, sample and test hemp but does not state the unit. Verify on the current NDDA fee schedule / by NDDA enquiry.
Part 1 §1.5 / Part 7Hemp = cannabis sativa L. with total THC “determined by the commissioner”; total THC = THCa × 0.877 + delta-9; 0.3% fixed in the definitionCorrectedhttps://www.legis.nd.gov/cencode/t04-1c18-1.pdfSubsection numbers corrected: hemp §4.1-18.1-01(4); hemp commodity/product §4.1-18.1-01(5); tetrahydrocannabinol §4.1-18.1-01(10); total THC §4.1-18.1-01(11) = (THCa × 0.877) + delta-9 THC. §4.1-18.1-04.1 caps the commissioner's amount at no more than the 2018 Farm Bill and 7 CFR part 990 — ND does NOT fix 0.3% in the definition itself; 0.3% total THC is NDDA's applied amount (flower “less than 0.3% Total THC”).
Part 1 §1.5Delta-8, THC-O, HHC, THCP expressly excluded from “hemp commodity or product” (SB 2096, 2023); conversion ban; hemp-extract inhalants excluded; THCA flower under 0.3% total THCConfirmedhttps://www.legis.nd.gov/cencode/t04-1c18-1.pdfConfirmed in substance; subsection corrected to §4.1-18.1-01(5)(b): excludes (3) delta-8 products, (4) chemically derived cannabinoids incl. THC-O acetate, HHC, THCP, (5) commissioner-disapproved psychotropic products, and (2) a chemical compound extracted from hemp used to make an inhalant. Conversion ban §4.1-18.1-04.3 confirmed; sale ban §4.1-18.1-04.4(2) confirmed. NDDA: THCA flower must be under 0.3% total THC (e.g. 25% THCa × 0.877 + 0.2% = 22.13% total THC); most THCA products cannot meet the limit. THCA itself is not separately named in the prohibited list — illegality flows from the total-THC calculation.
Part 7 / Part 11Hemp testing/labelling/packaging summary; NDAC art. 7-20 eff. July 2023; “1.25 mg / 15:1 proposal is NOT law”Correctedhttps://ndlegis.gov/prod/acdata/pdf/7-20-01.pdfNDAC ch. 7-20-01 effective 1 July 2024, not July 2023. Testing §4.1-18.1-04.4(1) + NDAC §7-20-01-04 confirmed (COA on label/QR, independent-lab batch testing, contaminant-free). Labelling §7-20-01-02 and packaging §7-20-01-03 (plain, unique, tamper-evident, child-resistant, not attractive to children, Poison Prevention Packaging Act-compliant) confirmed in substance. Material correction: NDAC §7-20-01-01(2) defines “cannabinoid hemp” as total THC not exceeding 5 mg per serving AND total CBD:total THC ratio greater than 15:1 (or sum of non-THC cannabinoids at 15:1+ where CBD is not primary). The 15:1 ratio IS adopted law; the adopted figure is 5 mg, not 1.25 mg.
Part 7 / Part 12Minimum purchase age for lawful hemp products is 21+ (or 18+)Correctedhttps://ndlegis.gov/prod/acdata/pdf/7-20-01.pdfNo minimum purchase age for lawful hemp products appears in ch. 4.1-18.1 or NDAC 7-20-01. What exists: NDAC §7-20-01-02(1)(j)(5) label warnings (“This product is not for minors”, “Keep out of reach of children”) and §7-20-01-04(3)(a) (retailers may not market/entice/encourage minors). What “minors” means for this rule is not defined in the NDAC text read — Verify. Retailer 18+/21+ policies are private policies, not law. Intoxicating hemp is prohibited at any age, so there is no “21+ delta-8” rule in ND.
Part 7 / Part 12ND has a lawful THC-beverage categoryCorrectedhttps://www.legis.nd.gov/cencode/t04-1c18-1.pdfNo ND statute or NDAC section specifically authorising “THC beverages” was found. NDCC §4.1-18.1-04.4(5) (incorporating the Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act) provides non-FDA-approved hemp-derived products may not be sold as dietary supplements, food or beverage products, or marketed with medical claims. NDDA guidance deems a delta-9-only product psychotropic and not legal (FAQ 3, applying the commissioner's disapproval power, not a sentence naming delta-9-only products). Whether a non-intoxicating CBD/CBG beverage within the total-THC cap is an allowable edible under NDAC 7-20-01 remains Verify.
Part 7ND has adopted the federal 0.4 mg total-THC-per-container cap (PL 119-37 / 119-103)Unverifiedhttps://www.legis.nd.gov/cencode/t04-1c18-1.pdfVerify. No ND statute, NDAC rule or NDDA bulletin adopting the cap was located. Current ND law still caps the commissioner's amount by reference to the 2018 Farm Bill / 7 CFR part 990, NDDA still applies 0.3% total THC, and NDAC 7-20-01 (2024) uses 5 mg/serving / 15:1, not 0.4 mg/container. Partial overlap (total THC + delta-8/conversion/inhalant bans) is NOT adoption. No 2026 regular session existed to enact a conforming bill; any action would have to be a commissioner determination, NDAC amendment or special-session bill — none located.
Part 6 §6.6–6.7 / Part 7Hemp acreage: licensed acres = NASS acres; 2020 licensed acres ~3,400 vs ~5,000 (same 81 growers); current licensed-acre total knownCorrectedhttps://www.nass.usda.gov/Statistics_by_State/North_Dakota/Publications/Crop_Releases/Hemp_Release/2025/ND-hemp2504.pdfNever merge the series. These are survey grown/harvested acres, NOT NDDA licensed acres. No NDDA licensed-acre total for 2021–2025 was located; the 2020 licensed-acre conflict (~3,400 vs ~5,000) remains unresolved on primary sources. Historic secondary figures (81 growers in 2020, 8 processors as of 2023) are dated secondary only — do not quote as current.

Part 12 §12.4Expungement: ND has no general expungement (destruction) regime; marijuana sealing is petition-based, not automatic; §19-03.1-23(9) first offence ≤1 oz / ≤2 g THC, sealed if no further ch. 19-03.1 violation within 2 yearsConfirmedhttps://ndlegis.gov/assembly/69-2025/regular/documents/25-0100-02000.pdfConfirmed, and the broader bar is resolved on the 2025 enrolled text (HB 1030): §19-03.1-23(9) states only “if the person is not subsequently convicted within two years of a further violation of this chapter” — the older 2007 “and has not been convicted of any other criminal offense” limb does not appear in the current text as printed.   • SWO Commission size (five vs. four), full §81-06-01 text, SWO retail on the ND portion of the reservation, SWO buyer rules, SWO prior hemp-plan withdrawal/revocation history; MHA decriminalization resolution/date and Advisory Survey results; Spirit Lake / Standing Rock / Turtle Mountain current cannabis codes; any ND–tribal compact — current SWO Code/Regulations PDFs, SWO Medical Cannabis Commission; MHA Tribal Business Council resolutions; each tribe's current code site; ND Governor's office / Indian Affairs Commission; USDA AMS.
  • Landlord/tenant application of §19-24.1-32; public-housing/HUD position; CBD vape as “electronic smoking device” (§12.1-31-03); CBD pet-treat ingredient acceptance; veterinary prescribing authority; §19-24.1-32 firearm sentence; §15.1-19-23 current text; DPI statewide medical-marijuana guidance — current §19-24.1-32 text; ND case law; current HUD guidance; NDDA Pet Food Program; ND Board of Veterinary Medical Examiners / ch. 43-29; ND Department of Public Instruction.

Combined edition — scope

This combined file preserves the Run 1 file (Parts 0–6) unchanged in substance and appends the full Run 2 content (Parts 7–12), with Run 2 corrections shown as visible correction callouts before Part 7 — Run 1 text was not silently rewritten. The 3 November 2026 election had not occurred on 30 October 2026, so there is no 2026 cannabis result in this file. Federal baseline (as commissioned, not re-researched): split scheduling — state-licensed medical marijuana and FDA-approved products moved to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714), adult-use marijuana remains Schedule I; IRS 280E generally lifted for Schedule III medical activity only; hemp PL 119-37 §781 effective 12 November 2026, limited by PL 119-103 §2019 until 11 December 2026, after which the full total-THC hemp definition and the 0.4 mg total THC per container cap apply; USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing for hemp delayed to 31 December 2026; the federal medical marijuana appropriations rider runs through 11 December 2026; there is no federal cannabis banking law. No personal phone numbers or emails of individuals are reproduced; agency public contacts only.

Federal baseline — exactly as given in the commission (not re-researched)

  • Split scheduling: state-licensed MEDICAL marijuana and FDA-approved products moved to Schedule III effective 28 April 2026 (91 FR 22714). All adult-use marijuana remains Schedule I. The broader 2024 rescheduling proposal is not final.
  • IRS 280E is generally lifted for Schedule III medical activity; adult-use remains fully subject to 280E.
  • Hemp: PL 119-37 §781 takes effect 12 November 2026, but PL 119-103 §2019 limits it until 11 December 2026. After 11 December 2026, the full total-THC hemp definition and the 0.4 mg total THC per container cap apply.
  • USDA enforcement of DEA-registered lab testing for hemp is delayed to 31 December 2026.
  • The federal medical marijuana appropriations rider runs through 11 December 2026.
  • There is no federal cannabis banking law.

North Dakota application: North Dakota's medical marijuana programme is state-licensed (HHS Division of Medical Marijuana registers compassion centers — manufacturing facilities and dispensaries), so on the given baseline its manufacturing facilities' and dispensaries' state-licensed medical activity falls in the Schedule III / 280E-lifted category, while any non-medical possession remains Schedule I federally and fully subject to 280E. Verify: whether any tribal medical programme counts as “state-licensed” for this purpose (Part 10, Run 2). Verify: whether the North Dakota State Board of Pharmacy objected to, or has yet implemented, the federal move under NDCC §19-03.1-02(4) — no Board order was located, and the ND Schedule I text read still lists marijuana and tetrahydrocannabinols. See Part 1, §§1.2.3 and 1.9 gaps. North Dakota law follows federal schedule changes only through that §19-03.1-02(4) mechanism (30 days from Federal Register publication, unless the Board objects, publishes reasons, and holds a hearing).

No contradiction of the federal baseline was found in the North Dakota official sources read for Run 1.

The Secretary of State's Measures-on-Ballot page for the 2026 General Election lists only Constitutional Measure 1 and Constitutional Measure 2 — neither about cannabis. Any “2026 result” claim would be false.
  • North Dakota's Legislative Assembly meets in regular session in odd-numbered years. The 69th Assembly sat in 2025; the next regular session is 2027. There is no 2026 regular session.
  • Place-name discipline: Fargo, ND is not Moorhead, MN; Grand Forks, ND is not East Grand Forks, MN; Williston, Dickinson, Jamestown, Grafton, Rugby and Devils Lake in ND are not their same-name counterparts elsewhere. North Dakota townships share names across counties; always give the county.
